\[ \mathrm{Var}(w_{ji}x_i) = \mathrm{Var}(w_{ji})\,\mathrm{Var}(x_i) \]

Assuming that the weights and the inputs are i.i.d., the \(I\) terms are independent and have the same variance, so

\[ \mathrm{Var}(h_j) = I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w_i)\,\mathrm{Var}(x_i) \]

The output variance is the input variance scaled by \(I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w_i)\). The output of one layer is the input of the next, so the variance stays the same across layers only if

\[ I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w) = 1 \]

that is \(w \sim N(0,\ 1/n_{in})\) with \(n_{in} = I\); the second argument is the variance. The same reasoning for the gradient, which a linear neuron collects from the \(n_{out}\) units it feeds,

\[ \delta_i = \sum_{j=1}^{n_{out}} w_{ji}\,\delta_j \]

gives \(n_{out}\,\mathrm{Var}(w) = 1\). Both conditions hold only if \(n_{in} = n_{out}\), so Glorot & Bengio propose the compromise

\[ w \sim N\Bigl(0,\ \frac{2}{n_{in} + n_{out}}\Bigr) \]

The linear neuron is a strong assumption, but tanh is close to linear around zero and in practice the formula works.

Ответ идёт строка за строкой по слайдам 31–32 лекции 3, с теми же буквами: \(I\), \(n_{in}\), \(n_{out}\). Проверяют четыре вещи: допущения названы (линейный нейрон, нулевое среднее у входов и весов, i.i.d.); есть формула с тремя слагаемыми и шаг про сумму; названы оба условия, для выхода и для градиента; итог объяснён как компромисс между ними. Одной итоговой формулы мало: вопрос начинается со слова derive.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 2 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A linear neuron has \(I = 400\) inputs, i.i.d. with zero mean and variance 1. Its weights are drawn independently from \(N(0,\ \sigma^2 = 0.01)\). Compute the variance of its output \(h_j\).

Show model answer
\[ \mathrm{Var}(h_j) = I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w_i)\,\mathrm{Var}(x_i) \] \[ \mathrm{Var}(h_j) = 400 \cdot 0.01 \cdot 1 = 4 \]

The variance of the output is 4 times the variance of the input. To keep it the same the weights need \(\mathrm{Var}(w) = 1/400 = 0.0025\).

Формула — итог слайда 31 лекции 3. Ловушка в записи \(N(0,\ \cdot)\): на слайдах второй аргумент — дисперсия. Если принять 0.01 за \(\sigma\), получится \(400 \cdot 0.0001 = 0.04\), ошибка в сто раз.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 3 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A fully connected layer with tanh activation has 300 inputs and 100 outputs. Its weights are drawn from a normal distribution with the Xavier (Glorot & Bengio) variance that takes both the forward and the backward pass into account. Compute the standard deviation of the weights.

Show model answer
\[ w \sim N\Bigl(0,\ \frac{2}{n_{in} + n_{out}}\Bigr) \] \[ \mathrm{Var}(w) = \frac{2}{300 + 100} = 0.005 \] \[ \sigma = \sqrt{0.005} \approx 0.0707 \]

Три типичные потери. Ответить 0.005: это дисперсия, а спрашивают стандартное отклонение. Взять \(2/300\): это формула He. Взять \(1/300\): это первая формула слайда 32 лекции 3, \(N(0,\ 1/n_{in})\), она следит только за прямым проходом, а в вопросе названы оба.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 4 из 8 · открыть в уроке

The same layer (300 inputs, 100 outputs) now uses ReLU and He uniform initialization, \(w \sim U(-a, a)\). Compute \(a\).

Show model answer

He: \(\mathrm{Var}(w) = 2/n_{in} = 2/300\). A uniform distribution on \([-a, a]\) has variance \(a^2/3\), therefore

\[ a = \sqrt{\frac{6}{n_{in}}} = \sqrt{0.02} \approx 0.1414 \]

Формулу с шестёркой не обязательно помнить: достаточно знать, что у \(U(-a, a)\) дисперсия \(a^2/3\), и приравнять её к \(2/n_{in}\). Число выходов 100 здесь лишнее — его дают, чтобы проверить, не перепутаны ли He и Xavier. Равномерного варианта на слайдах нет: задача — на случай, когда распределение задано в условии.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 5 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Why can the weights of a Feed Forward Neural Network not all be initialized to zero, or to the same constant?

Show model answer

With identical weights all neurons of a hidden layer compute the same output and receive the same gradient, so after every update they are still identical: the symmetry is never broken and the layer is equivalent to a single neuron.

With all-zero weights it is even worse. With tanh or ReLU hidden units the hidden outputs are \(g(0) = 0\), so the gradients of the next layer's weights, \(\delta_j h_i\), are zero, and the hidden \(\delta\) are zero because they are multiplied by zero weights. All the gradients of the weights are zero and no learning happens: only the output bias can move, so the network learns at most a constant. With sigmoid hidden units \(g(0) = 0.5\), so the gradients are not all zero, but they are identical within a layer, which is again the symmetry problem.

Random initialization breaks the symmetry; its scale must then be chosen so that the variance of activations and gradients is preserved across layers (Xavier, He). Biases can be initialized to zero.

Ключевые слова ответа: same output, same gradient, symmetry. На слайде 30 лекции 3 про нули сказано: "All gradient would be zero". Эту фразу стоит привести, но одной её мало: она верна только для нулей, и то не для каждой активации, а при ненулевой константе градиенты не нулевые, а одинаковые.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 6 из 8 · открыть в уроке

All the layers of a Feed Forward Neural Network are initialized with \(w \sim N(0,\ \sigma^2 = 0.01)\), whatever their size. Explain why this is good for small networks but might be a problem for deeper ones. What happens to the gradient if the weights start too small or too large? What do Xavier and He initialization propose instead?

Show model answer

For a linear neuron with \(I\) zero-mean i.i.d. inputs the output variance is the input variance scaled by \(I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w)\). With a fixed variance the scale factor is \(0.01 \cdot I\): it equals 1 only for \(I = 100\), it is smaller for narrower layers and larger for wider ones.

In a small network a factor different from 1 is applied only a couple of times and training still works. In a deep network it is applied at every layer and compounds exponentially. If the weights start too small, the signal and its gradient shrink at every layer (vanishing gradient). If they start too large, the gradient grows at every layer until it is too large to be useful (exploding gradient); with sigmoid or tanh units the neurons saturate instead, and convergence can take very long.

Xavier and He initialization tie the variance to the size of the layer, so that the factor is 1. Xavier: \(w \sim N(0,\ 1/n_{in})\) or, taking the gradient into account as well, \(w \sim N\bigl(0,\ 2/(n_{in} + n_{out})\bigr)\). He, for rectified linear units: \(w \sim N(0,\ 2/n_{in})\).

Ответ идёт по строкам слайда 30 лекции 3: вердикт про \(N(0,\ \sigma^2 = 0.01)\), потом фразы "too small" и "too large", потом три формулы слайда 32 лекции 3. Сами вердикты — только половина ответа, вторая половина — причина: множитель \(I \cdot \mathrm{Var}(w)\) не равен 1 и перемножается по слоям.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 7 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A network has ReLU layers of equal width \(n\), zero biases and weights \(w \sim N(0,\ 1/n)\), so that \(n\,\mathrm{Var}(w) = 1\). By what factor is the standard deviation of the pre-activations multiplied after 10 such layers (for instance from layer 1 to layer 11)?

Show model answer

ReLU zeroes the negative half of the pre-activations, so \(\E[h^2] = \tfrac12 \mathrm{Var}(z)\) and each layer multiplies the variance by \(\tfrac12\,n\,\mathrm{Var}(w) = \tfrac12\).

After 10 layers the variance is multiplied by

\[ \left(\tfrac12\right)^{10} = \tfrac{1}{1024} \]

and the standard deviation by

\[ \sqrt{\tfrac{1}{1024}} = \tfrac{1}{32} \approx 0.031 \]

The signal vanishes; He initialization, \(\mathrm{Var}(w) = 2/n\), restores a factor of 1.

Ловушка в словах standard deviation: множитель ½ относится к дисперсии, для стандартного отклонения это \(1/\sqrt2\) за слой. Ответ 1/1024 — правильная арифметика для не той величины.

Урок 25 · Ксерокопия ксерокопии · вопрос 8 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about weight initialization are correct? Select all that apply.

  • If all weights start from the same constant, all neurons of a hidden layer receive identical updates. Одинаковый выход и одинаковый градиент на каждом шаге: симметрия не ломается.
  • Xavier initialization must be sampled from a uniform distribution and He initialization from a normal one. Обе бывают и normal, и uniform. Формулы задают дисперсию, а не форму распределения.
  • He initialization uses \(\mathrm{Var}(w) = 2/n_{in}\) because ReLU zeroes about half of the pre-activations. От среднего квадрата остаётся половина, и двойка её компенсирует.
  • A proper initialization keeps activations and gradients stable during the whole training. Только в момент старта. Потом веса меняются; для этого нужна batch normalization.
  • For a layer with \(n_{in} = n_{out} = n\) the Xavier variance equals \(1/n\). \(2/(n + n) = 1/n\): для квадратного слоя прямое и обратное условия совпадают.
Show model answer

Correct: the first, the third and the fifth statement. The second confuses the variance with the shape of the distribution; the fourth ignores that weights drift away from their initial statistics as soon as training starts.

В вопросах «select all» проверяй каждое утверждение отдельно и отмечай только то, что можешь обосновать одной фразой. Здесь три обоснования: одинаковые поправки, половина среднего квадрата, \(2/(n + n) = 1/n\).

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 1 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Write the Batch Normalization transform applied to the pre-activations \(x_1, \dots, x_m\) of one unit over a mini-batch (a) at training time and (b) at test time. Explain why the learnable parameters \(\gamma\) and \(\beta\) are introduced.

Show model answer

(a) Training: mean and stddev are computed for each mini-batch, separately for each unit; the unit's pre-activation is normalized (mean subtraction, stddev division), then scaled and shifted.

\[ \mu_B = \frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m} x_i \] \[ \sigma_B^2 = \frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(x_i - \mu_B)^2 \] \[ \hat x_i = \frac{x_i - \mu_B}{\sqrt{\sigma_B^2 + \epsilon}} \] \[ y_i = \gamma\,\hat x_i + \beta \equiv \mathrm{BN}_{\gamma,\beta}(x_i) \]

Backpropagation takes the normalization into account. Meanwhile running averages are updated: \(\mu_{\mathrm{run}} \leftarrow (1-\alpha)\mu_{\mathrm{run}} + \alpha\mu_B\), and the same for \(\sigma^2_{\mathrm{run}}\).

(b) Test: the global mean / stddev are used instead of the mini-batch ones; these global statistics are the running averages estimated during training. They are frozen, so the output is a deterministic function of the single input and does not depend on the other samples.

\[ y = \gamma\,\frac{x - \mu_{\mathrm{run}}}{\sqrt{\sigma^2_{\mathrm{run}} + \epsilon}} + \beta \]

\(\gamma\) and \(\beta\): normalisation alone would force every unit to zero mean and unit variance and would limit the representational power of the layer. The scale and shift step is a linear transformation that lets the network decide (learn) how much normalization it needs: it can give each unit any mean and scale, and even undo the normalisation with \(\gamma = \sigma\), \(\beta = \mu\). These become two directly learnable quantities, decoupled from the weights of the preceding layers.

В полном ответе четыре вещи: все четыре шага с \(\epsilon\) под корнем; слова «for each unit, over the mini-batch»; другой источник статистики на тесте; и объяснение, зачем нужны \(\gamma\), \(\beta\). Слова слайдов, которые стоит повторить: "global mean / stddev", "running averages", "the network can decide (learn) how much normalization" (лекция 3, слайды 35–36). Фраза «to restore the original distribution» сама по себе ничего не объясняет: скажи про representational power.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 2 из 8 · открыть в уроке

During training a unit receives the mini-batch of pre-activations \(x = (2,\ 4,\ 4,\ 6)\). Its Batch Normalization parameters are \(\gamma = 3\), \(\beta = -1\); neglect \(\epsilon\). Compute the output of the BN layer for the sample with \(x = 6\).

Show model answer
\[ \mu_B = \frac{2 + 4 + 4 + 6}{4} = 4 \] \[ \sigma_B^2 = \frac{4 + 0 + 0 + 4}{4} = 2, \quad \sigma_B = 1.414 \] \[ \hat x = \frac{6 - 4}{1.414} = 1.414 \] \[ y = 3 \cdot 1.414 - 1 = 3.243 \]

Две типичные потери: деление на \(m - 1\) (тогда \(\sigma_B^2 = 2.67\) и ответ 2.674) и забытый корень (деление на дисперсию 2 вместо 1.414 даёт ровно 2).

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 3 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A fully connected layer with 64 units is followed by a Batch Normalization layer. How many trainable parameters does the Batch Normalization layer add to the model?

Show model answer

One \(\gamma\) and one \(\beta\) per unit: \(2 \cdot 64 = 128\) trainable parameters. The layer also stores a running mean and a running variance per unit, \(2 \cdot 64 = 128\) non-trainable parameters, 256 in total.

Смотри на слово в вопросе: trainable — 128, total — 256. Число входов слоя на ответ не влияет.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 4 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about Batch Normalization at inference (test) time are correct? Select all that apply.

  • The mean and variance used are the running averages accumulated during training. Да: формулы (79)–(80) копят их всё обучение, на тесте они заморожены.
  • The mean and variance are computed on the test mini-batch. Тогда ответ для примера зависел бы от соседей, а для одного примера слой выдал бы константу \(\beta\).
  • The output for a sample does not depend on the other samples processed together with it. Именно ради этого статистику замораживают.
  • \(\gamma\) and \(\beta\) are re-estimated on the test data. Это обученные параметры. На тестовых данных в модели не меняется ничего.
  • The layer is a fixed affine function of its input and can be merged into the preceding linear layer. \(y = a x + c\) с константами \(a\) и \(c\).
Show model answer

Correct: the first, the third and the fifth. At test time the global statistics estimated with running averages are used: \(y = \gamma (x - \mu_{\mathrm{run}}) / \sqrt{\sigma^2_{\mathrm{run}} + \epsilon} + \beta\), where everything except \(x\) is a constant fixed after training.

Вопрос проверяет одну идею с трёх сторон: на тесте BN детерминирован. Если понятна причина, все пять вариантов решаются без запоминания.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 5 из 8 · открыть в уроке

The running mean of a Batch Normalization layer is initialised to 0 and updated as \(\mu_{\mathrm{run}} \leftarrow (1 - \alpha)\mu_{\mathrm{run}} + \alpha \mu_B\) with \(\alpha = 0.1\). The first three mini-batches have mini-batch means 2, 6 and 4. Compute the running mean after the third update.

Show model answer
\[ 0.9 \cdot 0 + 0.1 \cdot 2 = 0.2 \] \[ 0.9 \cdot 0.2 + 0.1 \cdot 6 = 0.78 \] \[ 0.9 \cdot 0.78 + 0.1 \cdot 4 = 1.102 \]

Ответ далёк от настоящего среднего 4: скользящее среднее стартует с нуля и разгоняется медленно. Если в задаче \(\alpha\) стоит при старом значении, а не при новом, — это запись Keras; прочитай формулу в условии.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 6 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A fully connected layer computes the pre-activations \(x = W h + b\) and is followed by Batch Normalization. (a) Why is the bias \(b\) redundant? (b) Why does Batch Normalization work poorly with very small mini-batches, and what is its output for a mini-batch of a single sample at training time?

Show model answer

(a) BN subtracts the mini-batch mean. Adding the same constant \(b\) to every \(x_i\) shifts \(\mu_B\) by \(b\) and leaves \(x_i - \mu_B\) and \(\sigma_B^2\) unchanged, so \(\mathrm{BN}(x + b) = \mathrm{BN}(x)\). The shift is provided by \(\beta\) instead.

(b) \(\mu_B\) and \(\sigma_B^2\) are estimates from \(m\) samples; their noise grows as \(m\) decreases (standard error \(\sigma/\sqrt{m}\)), so the output for a sample depends strongly on its random companions, and the running averages become noisy as well. With \(m = 1\): \(\mu_B = x\), \(\sigma_B^2 = 0\), \(\hat x = 0\), so the output is \(\beta\) for every input.

В (a) нужна одна строка алгебры, а не слова «BN нормализует». В (b) нужны слова noisy estimates и конкретный ответ «\(\beta\)» для одного примера.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 7 из 8 · открыть в уроке

What is internal covariate shift, and how does Batch Normalization cope with it? State where the Batch Normalization layer is placed in the network.

Show model answer

Networks converge faster if their inputs have been whitened (zero mean, unit variances) and are uncorrelated. The same issue appears inside the network: during training the distribution of the inputs of each hidden layer keeps changing, because the weights of the preceding layers are updated. This is internal covariate shift, so normalization is useful also at the level of hidden layers.

Batch Normalization normalizes each unit's pre-activation with the mean and stddev of the current mini-batch. This forces the activations to take values on a unit Gaussian (zero mean, unit variance) at the beginning of the training; a learnable scale \(\gamma\) and shift \(\beta\) then let the network decide how much normalization it needs. The BatchNorm layer is added after fully connected (or convolutional) layers and before the nonlinearities: Fully Connected → Batch Norm → ReLU. It can be interpreted as doing preprocessing at every layer of the network, but integrated into the network itself in a differentiable way.

Ответ собран из формулировок слайдов 33–34 лекции 3: их стоит узнавать и уметь повторить. Covariate shift без слова internal — другое явление: распределение входов сети на обучении и на тесте разное.

Урок 26 · Оценка по кривой · вопрос 8 из 8 · открыть в уроке

List four effects that Batch Normalization has been shown to have on the training of a network, and give a one-sentence reason for each.

Show model answer

1. It improves the gradient flow through the network: the pre-activations stay in the non-saturating range of the nonlinearity, so the gradients do not vanish.

2. It allows using higher learning rates (faster learning): the layer output does not depend on the scale of the incoming weights, \(\mathrm{BN}(c\,x) = \mathrm{BN}(x)\) for any \(c \gt 0\), and larger weights get smaller gradients.

3. It reduces the strong dependence on weight initialization: the scale of the signal entering the nonlinearity is set by \(\gamma\) and \(\beta\), not by the initial weights.

4. It acts as a form of regularization, slightly reducing the need for dropout: at training time the output for a sample depends on the other samples of its mini-batch, which adds noise.

Четыре пункта — это список "Has shown to" (лекция 3, слайд 37). Причин на слайде нет, а без них ответ превращается в перечисление.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 1 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Compare batch gradient descent, stochastic gradient descent (SGD) and mini-batch gradient descent. For each of them write the gradient used for one weight update, and explain the trade-off.

Show model answer

Batch gradient descent uses all \(N\) samples:

\[ \pd{E(w)}{w} = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N} \pd{E(x_n, w)}{w} \]

SGD uses a single sample:

\[ \pd{E(w)}{w} \approx \pd{E(x_n, w)}{w} \]

Mini-batch gradient descent uses a subset of \(M \lt N\) samples:

\[ \pd{E(w)}{w} \approx \frac{1}{M}\sum_{n \in \text{mini-batch}} \pd{E(x_n, w)}{w} \]

Batch: the gradient is exact, but every update costs a pass over the whole dataset (one update per epoch), which is impractical for large \(N\). SGD: \(N\) cheap updates per epoch; the estimate is unbiased but has high variance, so the trajectory is noisy. Mini-batch: still unbiased, with variance reduced by a factor \(M\) (noise \(\propto 1/\sqrt{M}\)); it is a good trade-off between variance and computation and exploits GPU parallelism. Because of the \(1/\sqrt{M}\) law, larger batches give diminishing returns.

Проверяют три вещи: три формулы, слова unbiased / high variance и то, что mini-batch — компромисс между дисперсией и вычислениями. Закон \(1/\sqrt{M}\) — то, что отличает отличный ответ от хорошего.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 2 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A training set contains \(N = 50\,000\) samples and the mini-batch size is \(M = 32\). How many weight updates (iterations) are performed in one epoch?

Show model answer
\[ \left\lceil \frac{50\,000}{32} \right\rceil = \lceil 1562.5 \rceil = 1563 \]

There are 1562 full mini-batches and one last mini-batch with the remaining 16 samples.

Ловушка в округлении: 1562 — это без последнего неполного mini-batch. Если в задаче спросят число обновлений за 10 эпох, умножь на 10.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 3 из 8 · открыть в уроке

A step-decay schedule starts from \(\eta_0 = 0.1\) and halves the learning rate every 10 epochs. What is the learning rate at epoch \(t = 25\)?

Show model answer
\[ \eta_{25} = \eta_0 \cdot \mathrm{drop}^{\lfloor 25/10 \rfloor} = 0.1 \cdot 0.5^{2} = 0.025 \]

Показатель степени — целая часть \(t/s\), то есть 2, а не 2.5. С показателем 2.5 получится 0.0177 — это уже не step decay.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 4 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Adam is run with \(\beta_1 = 0.9\), \(\beta_2 = 0.999\), \(\eta = 0.001\), \(m_0 = v_0 = 0\) and a negligible \(\varepsilon\). The first gradient of a weight is \(g_1 = 10\). Compute the first update \(\Delta w\) of that weight.

Show model answer
\[ m_1 = 0.1 \cdot 10 = 1, \quad v_1 = 0.001 \cdot 10^2 = 0.1 \] \[ \hat m_1 = \frac{1}{1 - 0.9} = 10 \] \[ \hat v_1 = \frac{0.1}{1 - 0.999} = 100 \] \[ \Delta w = -\eta\,\frac{\hat m_1}{\sqrt{\hat v_1}} = -0.001 \cdot \frac{10}{10} = -0.001 \]

The first step equals \(-\eta \cdot \sign(g_1)\), whatever the size of the gradient. The bias correction is needed because \(m\) and \(v\) start from zero, so the first running averages underestimate the true moments; dividing by \(1 - \beta^{k}\) removes this bias.

Если вышло −0.00316, забыта bias correction. Если +0.001 — потерян минус: шаг идёт против градиента.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 5 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.

  • A learning-rate schedule sets the global \(\eta\) as a function of the epoch, without looking at the gradients. Это определение расписания: функция времени, одна на все веса.
  • Momentum is a learning-rate schedule. В формуле momentum \(\eta\) постоянен. Меняется направление шага: к градиенту добавляется накопленная скорость.
  • Adam rescales the step of every weight using running averages of its gradients. Среднее градиентов в числителе, корень из среднего квадратов в знаменателе — отдельно для каждого веса.
  • Cosine annealing \(\eta_t = \frac{\eta_0}{2}(1 + \cos\frac{\pi t}{T})\) restarts by itself because the cosine is periodic. На отрезке от 0 до \(T\) это монотонное убывание до нуля. Рестарт — отдельное действие: сброс \(t\).
  • With Adam a learning-rate schedule is useless. Adam распределяет шаг между весами, а общий \(\eta\) по-прежнему задаёшь ты. Расписание ставят и поверх Adam.
Show model answer

Statements A and C are correct. A schedule is a function \(\eta_t\) of time; momentum changes the direction of the update by accumulating past gradients; adaptive learning-rate methods (RMSprop, Adam) rescale the step per parameter. The three mechanisms are independent and are usually combined. Cosine annealing as written decreases monotonically from \(\eta_0\) to 0 on \([0, T]\); a restart is a separate reset of \(t\).

Вопрос на различение трёх ручек. Конспект их смешивает, поэтому заученная оттуда фраза здесь стоит баллов.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 6 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Write the gradient-descent update rule with momentum. Explain why momentum helps when the error surface has a narrow valley (a ravine).

Show model answer

As seen in class:

\[ \begin{aligned} w^{k+1} = w^{k} &- \eta\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k}} \\ &- \alpha\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k-1}} \end{aligned} \]

The standard (velocity) form of the same idea accumulates all past gradients, not only the previous one:

\[ \begin{aligned} v_k &= \alpha\,v_{k-1} - \eta\,\nabla E(w_k) \\ w_{k+1} &= w_k + v_k \end{aligned} \]

with \(0 \le \alpha \lt 1\), typically 0.9. In a ravine a single learning rate is limited by the steep direction, so plain gradient descent either zig-zags across the valley or crawls along its flat bottom. The velocity is an exponentially weighted sum of past gradients: components that keep the same sign (along the valley) add up, up to a factor \(1/(1-\alpha)\); components that change sign (across the valley) cancel. The same inertia helps to roll through small local minima and plateaus.

Формула слайда (лекция 2, слайд 24; лекция 3, слайд 38) плюс объяснение через «складываются — гасятся». Число \(1/(1-\alpha) = 10\) при \(\alpha = 0.9\) показывает, что ты понимаешь, откуда ускорение.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 7 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Write the Nesterov accelerated gradient update as seen in class and explain how it differs from momentum.

Show model answer

Nesterov accelerated gradient: make a jump as momentum, then adjust.

\[ w^{k+\frac{1}{2}} = w^{k} - \alpha\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k-1}} \] \[ w^{k+1} = w^{k} - \eta\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k+\frac{1}{2}}} \]

The first line is the momentum jump to an intermediate (look-ahead) point \(w^{k+\frac{1}{2}}\). The second line is the correction: a gradient step whose gradient is evaluated at the look-ahead point instead of \(w^k\). The correction is applied after the jump, so the whole update is

\[ \begin{aligned} w^{k+1} = w^{k} &- \alpha\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k-1}} \\ &- \eta\,\pd{E(w)}{w}\Big|_{w^{k+\frac{1}{2}}} \end{aligned} \]

Momentum has the same two terms, but both gradients are computed at points already visited, \(w^k\) and \(w^{k-1}\): it measures the gradient first and then jumps. Nesterov jumps first and measures the gradient where the jump lands. If the jump overshoots, that gradient already points back, so the step is corrected immediately and not one iteration later: fewer oscillations, less overshooting. In the standard notation with a velocity:

\[ \begin{aligned} v_k &= \alpha\,v_{k-1} - \eta\,\nabla E(w_k + \alpha\,v_{k-1}) \\ w_{k+1} &= w_k + v_k \end{aligned} \]

Ждут две строки слайда 39 лекции 3 и мысль the gradient is evaluated after the jump. Запись через скорость — запасной вариант, если формула слайда не вспомнится.

Урок 27 · Ложка супа и тяжёлый шар · вопрос 8 из 8 · открыть в уроке

Why are adaptive learning rates useful when training a deep network? Name three algorithms that implement them and say in one sentence what each of them does.

Show model answer

Neurons in each layer learn differently: gradient magnitudes vary across layers, and early layers get "vanishing gradients". A single learning rate is therefore too large for some weights and too small for others; ideally every weight should have its own learning rate, adapted during training.

Algorithms (any three):

  • Rprop (Riedmiller and Braun): uses only the sign of the gradient and keeps a separate step size for every weight, increased while the sign stays the same and decreased when it flips; it needs full-batch gradients.
  • AdaGrad (Duchi et al.): divides the learning rate of every weight by the square root of the sum of all its past squared gradients, so the step can only shrink.
  • RMSprop (Tieleman and Hinton): "SGD + Rprop" — divides the gradient by the square root of a running average of its squared values, so it works with mini-batches and the step does not shrink to zero.
  • AdaDelta (Zeiler): like RMSprop, and it also replaces the global learning rate with the root of a running average of the squared past updates, so no learning rate has to be set.
  • Adam (Kingma and Ba): RMSprop plus momentum — running averages of the gradient and of its square, with bias correction.

Первая половина ответа — строки слайда 40 лекции 3 почти дословно. Во второй хватит трёх имён, но к каждому нужно действие, а не только название. Годы не обязательны.
