Модуль 4 · Последовательности и память

40 вопросов · Конспект, гл. 4 · стр. 200–293 · слайды лекции 4

Урок 28 · Окно или память

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Sequences ("dynamic" datasets) can be modelled by memoryless models or by models with memory. Name the models of each family presented in the course and explain what distinguishes the two families.

Show model answer

Memoryless models: autoregressive models, which predict the next input as a weighted sum of a fixed number of previous inputs ("delay taps"), and feed-forward neural networks, which take the same window of past inputs and process it with non-linear hidden layers (they generalize autoregressive models). The prediction depends only on the window supplied at that step: nothing persists between steps, so an input older than the window cannot influence the output.

Models with memory: linear dynamical systems, hidden Markov models and recurrent neural networks. They keep a hidden state of fixed size, updated at every step by the same rule \(h^t = F(h^{t-1}, x^t)\), and compute the output from it. The state summarises the whole past, so there is no fixed horizon. LDS: continuous state, linear dynamics with Gaussian noise. HMM: discrete state (one of \(N\)), stochastic transitions and emissions. RNN: distributed continuous state, non-linear deterministic update.

Нужны две семьи с правильными членами, критерий различия «окно против состояния» и по строке на каждую модель с памятью. Частый промах — записать AR в модели с памятью: прошлое она видит, но не хранит.

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Why is it a bad idea to process a whole sequence with a standard feed-forward network by concatenating all time steps into one input vector? What does a model with a recurrent hidden state gain?

Show model answer

The input size of a feed-forward network is fixed, so sequences of different lengths cannot be handled (only cropped or padded). The number of first-layer weights grows linearly with the length, \(I\,(T+1)\,J\). Each time position has its own weights, so a pattern learned at one position must be learned again at every other one: nothing is shared across time.

A recurrent model applies the same update with the same parameters at every step and carries a fixed-size state. It accepts any length, its number of parameters does not depend on \(T\), and a pattern is recognised wherever it occurs.

Нужны все три пункта: переменная длина, рост числа весов, нет общих весов по времени. Пример с числами — 4 000 000 весов против 14 100 — делает ответ убедительным.

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

An autoregressive model of order 3 predicts \(\hat x^t = 0.7\,x^{t-1} + 0.2\,x^{t-2} + 0.1\,x^{t-3}\). The last three observations are \(x^{t-3} = 16\), \(x^{t-2} = 18\) and \(x^{t-1} = 20\). Compute \(\hat x^t\).

Show model answer
\[ \hat x^t = 0.7 \cdot 20 + 0.2 \cdot 18 + 0.1 \cdot 16 = 19.2 \]

The weights are non-negative and sum to 1, so the prediction is a weighted average of the window: it always lies between 16 and 20 and lags behind a rising trend. Signed weights can extrapolate: \(a = (2, -1)\) gives \(2 \cdot 20 - 18 = 22\).

Вес 0.7 относится к самому свежему значению \(x^{t-1} = 20\). Если получилось 16.8, веса приложены в обратном порядке.

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

In what sense does a feed-forward neural network "generalize" an autoregressive model? Why are both called memoryless, and what is their main limitation?

Show model answer

Both read the same delay taps, a window of the last \(p\) values. The autoregressive model combines them linearly; the feed-forward network passes them through non-linear hidden layers, so it can represent non-linear relations. With no hidden layer and a linear output the network reduces exactly to AR(\(p\)).

Both are memoryless because the prediction depends only on the window given at that step; no internal state is carried from one step to the next. Main limitation: a hard horizon. Information older than \(p\) steps cannot influence the output, whatever the weights, and a longer context needs a larger input layer (\(p \cdot I \cdot J\) first-layer weights). On the other hand, windows are independent, so training and inference parallelise easily.

Опасное место — конспект (стр. 208–209), где AR и TDNN различаются «прошлыми выходами против прошлых входов». Отвечай по слайду: вход один и тот же, разница в нелинейных скрытых слоях.

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

List the properties that characterize recurrent neural networks as models with memory. Explain why the distributed hidden state of an RNN stores information about the past more efficiently than the hidden state of a hidden Markov model.

Show model answer

An RNN uses a hidden layer as memory; the memory is updated by a non-linear transformation; the output is computed from the hidden state (and possibly from the input); the memory is a distributed, continuous representation.

A hidden Markov model is, at each step, in exactly one of its \(N\) states, so its state carries at most \(\log_2 N\) bits about the past. Remembering 100 bits would need \(2^{100} \approx 10^{30}\) states and about \(10^{60}\) transition probabilities, with \(O(N^2)\) work per step. In an RNN the information is spread over many units: \(H\) binary units already distinguish \(2^H\) states, so the capacity grows linearly with the number of units (about 100 units for 100 bits). The non-linear update also allows complex state changes that a linear system cannot express.

Ответ держится на двух вещах: четыре свойства (лекция 4, слайд 6) и количественный аргумент \(\log_2 N\). Без бит и степеней двойки ответ звучит как лозунг «RNN мощнее».

Урок 28 · Окно или память · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.

  • Autoregressive models and feed-forward networks applied to a window of past inputs are memoryless models. Обе модели видят прошлое только через окно; состояния между шагами нет.
  • An autoregressive model feeds its own past predictions back as inputs and therefore accumulates errors, while a feed-forward network on a window uses observed values. На шаг вперёд обе модели читают наблюдённые значения. Свои прогнозы на вход получает любая модель окна, но только в замкнутом цикле, и тогда ошибки копятся у обеих.
  • In the linear state update \(h^t = A h^{t-1} + B x^t\), an input received \(k\) steps ago enters the current state multiplied by \(A^k\). Разверни рекурсию: каждый шаг назад добавляет множитель \(A\).
  • Since an RNN is a deterministic system, its output cannot be a probability distribution. Детерминировано состояние. Выход часто — softmax, и cross-entropy на нём — maximum likelihood.
  • For a model with a window of length \(p\), an input older than \(p\) steps cannot influence the prediction, whatever the weights. Этого значения просто нет среди входов: жёсткий горизонт.
Show model answer

Statements 1, 3 and 5 are correct. Error accumulation comes from closing the loop (feeding the model its own forecasts) and affects linear and non-linear window models alike. An RNN has no latent random variables, but its output layer can be a softmax over classes or words, trained by maximum likelihood (cross-entropy).

Утверждения 2 и 4 повторяют конспект (стр. 209 и 231) — поэтому их и стоит отличать с первого взгляда.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Describe the structure of the recurrent network of the lecture with ordinary hidden units \(h_j\) and state units \(c_b\), and write the equations of the output, of a hidden unit and of a state unit at time \(t\). Which units implement the memory of the network?

Show model answer

The inputs \(x_{1,n}^t, \dots, x_{I,n}^t\), a bias "1" and the previous values of the state units \(c_1^{t-1}, \dots, c_B^{t-1}\) all feed the hidden layer, which contains the units \(h_j^t\) and \(c_b^t\). The output unit reads all hidden units and a bias. Each \(c_b^t\) is fed back, with a delay of one step, as the input \(c_b^{t-1}\) of the next step.

\[ \begin{aligned} g^t(x_n|w) = g\Big(&\sum_{j=0}^{J} w_{1j}^{(2)} h_j^t(\cdot) \\ &+ \sum_{b=0}^{B} v_{1b}^{(2)} c_b^t(\cdot)\Big) \end{aligned} \] \[ \begin{aligned} h_j^t(\cdot) = h_j\Big(&\sum_{i=0}^{I} w_{ji}^{(1)} x_{i,n}^t \\ &+ \sum_{b=0}^{B} v_{jb}^{(1)} c_b^{t-1}\Big) \end{aligned} \] \[ \begin{aligned} c_b^t(\cdot) = c_b\Big(&\sum_{i=0}^{I} w_{bi}^{(1)} x_{i,n}^t \\ &+ \sum_{b'=0}^{B} v_{bb'}^{(1)} c_{b'}^{t-1}\Big) \end{aligned} \]

Here \(w\) are feed-forward weights, \(v\) weights leaving the state units, \((1)\) and \((2)\) the layers, index 0 the bias. Only the state units \(c_b\) implement the memory: the \(h_j\) are recomputed at every step and nothing of them is kept.

Что важно показать: выход читает текущее \(c^t\), а не \(c^{t-1}\); веса из состояния — \(v\), прямые — \(w\); петля идёт с задержкой на шаг. Без этой петли нейроны состояния ничем не отличаются от остальных, и сеть превращается в сеть прямого распространения.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

A vanilla RNN reads input vectors of size \(D = 100\), has a hidden state of size \(H = 64\) and a softmax output layer with \(K = 10\) classes. The recurrent layer and the output layer each have one bias vector. How many trainable parameters does the model have?

Show model answer

\(W_x\): \(64 \cdot 100 = 6400\); \(W_h\): \(64 \cdot 64 = 4096\); \(b\): 64; \(W_y\): \(10 \cdot 64 = 640\); \(c\): 10. Total: 11 210. The number does not depend on the length of the sequences, because the same weights are used at every time step.

Две частые ошибки: взять \(W_h\) размером \(H \times D\) вместо \(H \times H\) и умножить всё на длину последовательности. Если задача про PyTorch, вспомни два вектора смещения: у nn.RNN(100, 64) без выходного слоя 10 624 параметра.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

What does it mean that the weights of an RNN are shared across time? Give three consequences of weight sharing.

Show model answer

The same cell — the same \(W_x\), \(W_h\) and \(b\) — is applied at every time step; the unrolled network contains \(T\) copies of one set of parameters, not \(T\) sets. Consequences: (1) the network can process sequences of any length, also longer than those seen in training; (2) the number of parameters, \(HD + H^2 + H\) plus the output layer, does not depend on the sequence length; (3) a pattern learned at one position is recognised at any position of the sequence.

Самое сильное следствие — первое: без разделения весов для шага \(T + 1\) весов просто нет. Ответ «веса одинаковые» без следствий слаб. Как обучать общие веса, разобрано в уроке 30.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Why does the state update of an RNN need a non-linearity? Describe what happens to the memory of a linear RNN \(h_t = W_h h_{t-1} + W_x x_t\), and what a tanh changes.

Show model answer

Unrolling the linear recursion gives

\[ h_t = W_h^{t} h_0 + \sum_{k=1}^{t} W_h^{\,t-k} W_x x_k . \]

(1) An input \(t-k\) steps back is multiplied by the matrix power \(W_h^{t-k}\): its effect decays, explodes or oscillates geometrically, depending on whether the eigenvalues of \(W_h\) have modulus below or above 1 (negative or complex eigenvalues make it oscillate). (2) Superposition: the contributions of the inputs only add up and cannot interact; the effect of "bad" is the same with or without a preceding "not", so negation cannot be represented. With tanh the state stays in \((-1, 1)\), the effect of an input depends on the current state, and for a recurrent weight above 1 a unit has stable fixed points (attractors) that keep a bit indefinitely: "non-linear dynamics allows complex hidden state updates".

Сильный ответ называет оба следствия линейности — геометрическое эхо и суперпозицию — и хотя бы одно, что даёт \(\tanh\). Аттракторы — дополнение сверх слайдов: хорошая иллюстрация, но не обязательная часть ответа.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

A linear recurrent unit \(h_t = 0.9\,h_{t-1} + x_t\) starts from \(h_0 = 0\), receives \(x_1 = 1\) and then only zeros. Compute \(h_{11}\).

Show model answer

\(h_t = 0.9^{\,t-1}\) for \(t \ge 1\), so \(h_{11} = 0.9^{10} \approx 0.349\). After 50 steps only \(0.9^{50} \approx 0.005\) of the pulse is left: the memory of a linear unit with \(|v| \lt 1\) fades geometrically.

Частая ошибка — показатель на единицу больше: \(0.9^{11} \approx 0.31\). Импульс пришёл на шаге 1, поэтому до шага 11 его умножили на \(0.9\) десять раз.

Урок 29 · Петля во времени · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about recurrent neural networks are correct? Select all that apply.

  • The hidden state \(h_t\) has the same size whatever the length of the sequence read so far. Верно: \(H\) чисел на любом шаге, поэтому память с потерями.
  • Given the same initial state and the same input sequence, an RNN always produces the same sequence of hidden states. Верно: RNN — детерминированная система, шума в уравнении состояния нет.
  • A state of \(H\) units, even if each takes only two values, can distinguish \(2^H\) configurations, while an HMM with \(N\) states holds at most \(\log_2 N\) bits. Верно: это и есть «efficiently» распределённого состояния.
  • Since RNNs are Turing complete, a vanilla RNN trained with backpropagation through time can learn any algorithm. Теорема — о существовании весов при бесконечной точности; об обучении она молчит.
  • Removing the non-linearity keeps the hidden state bounded. Наоборот: границы даёт \(\tanh\); линейное состояние при \(|v| \gt 1\) растёт без предела.
  • In the unrolled network every time step has its own weight matrices. Копии в развёртке — одна и та же ячейка с одними весами.
Show model answer

The first three are correct. Turing completeness is an existence result (with unbounded precision) and says nothing about learnability; a linear state can grow without bound; the copies of the unrolled network share one set of weights.

Ловушка вопроса — пункт про Тьюринга: он звучит как следствие из цитаты на слайде, но цитата говорит «can compute», а не «can learn».

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад · вопрос 1 из 5 · открыть в уроке

What is Backpropagation Through Time? Explain why a recurrent network has to be unrolled before backpropagation can be applied, how the replicas of a shared weight are initialised and updated (write the update rule seen in class), and why the replicas stay equal.

Show model answer

BPTT is backpropagation applied to a recurrent network unrolled in time. In the folded network the state is fed back into the same units with a one-step delay, so the computational graph contains a loop and backpropagation has no last layer to start from. Unrolling for \(U\) steps writes one copy (replica) of the recurrent units per time step: the loop becomes a chain, a feed-forward network \(U\) layers deep in time whose layers all use the same weights. The forward pass runs through the \(U\) steps and stores all states; the backward pass runs from the last step to the first.

The replicas \(W_B^{t-u}\), \(V_B^{t-u}\), \(u = 0, \dots, U-1\), are initialised to the same values. Each replica receives its own gradient, and every replica is updated with the average of these gradients:

\[ W_B = W_B - \eta \cdot \frac{1}{U} \sum_{u=0}^{U-1} \pd{E}{W_B^{t-u}} \]

and the same for \(V_B\). All replicas start equal and receive the same update, so they stay equal: they remain one shared weight. Otherwise the network would turn into \(U\) different layers, which is no longer a recurrent network and cannot process sequences of other lengths. The true gradient of the shared weight is the sum of the replica gradients; the average only rescales the learning rate to \(\eta / U\).

Проверяют три вещи: зачем разворачивать (петля свёрнутой сети становится цепочкой, сетью, глубокой во времени), как обращаются с репликами (одинаковая инициализация и среднее градиентов — формула слайда 11 лекции 4) и почему копии остаются равными. Фраза «сумма — настоящий градиент, среднее — тот же шаг с \(\eta / U\)» отличает понимание от пересказа.

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад · вопрос 2 из 5 · открыть в уроке

A recurrent network is unrolled for \(U = 4\) steps. The gradients of the error with respect to the four replicas of the recurrent weight \(V_B\) (a scalar here) are \(0.2\), \(-0.1\), \(0.4\) and \(0.3\). Before the update \(V_B = 0.5\), and \(\eta = 0.1\). Compute \(V_B\) after one BPTT update as seen in class.

Show model answer

The average of the replica gradients:

\[ \frac{1}{U} \sum_{u=0}^{3} \pd{E}{V_B^{t-u}} = \frac{0.8}{4} = 0.2 \] \[ V_B = 0.5 - 0.1 \cdot 0.2 = 0.48 \]

All four replicas take the value 0.48.

С суммой вместо среднего вышло бы \(0.5 - 0.1 \cdot 0.8 = 0.42\): направление то же, шаг вчетверо длиннее. Слова "as seen in class" означают среднее со слайда 11 лекции 4.

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад · вопрос 3 из 5 · открыть в уроке

A recurrent unit with linear activation computes \(h^t = v\,h^{t-1} + w\,x^t\), with output \(y^t = h^t\), \(h^0 = 0\), \(v = 0.5\) and \(w = 1\). The input sequence is \(x^1 = 1\), \(x^2 = 0\); the target is 1 at both steps, and the error is

\[ E = \sum_{t=1}^{2} \tfrac12\,(y^t - 1)^2 . \]

Compute \(\partial E / \partial w\) with Backpropagation Through Time.

Show model answer

Forward: \(h^1 = 1\), \(h^2 = 0.5\); \(E^1 = 0\), \(E^2 = 0.125\).

Backward, with \(\delta^t = \partial E / \partial a^t\) and \(h' = 1\):

\[ \delta^2 = y^2 - 1 = -0.5 \] \[ \delta^1 = (y^1 - 1) + v\,\delta^2 = 0 - 0.25 = -0.25 \]

Replica gradients of \(w\): \(\delta^1 x^1 = -0.25\) and \(\delta^2 x^2 = 0\). Sum: \(\partial E / \partial w = -0.25\). Likewise \(\partial E / \partial v = \delta^1 h^0 + \delta^2 h^1 = -0.5\).

Ловушка — считать каждой копии только «свою» ошибку: на шаге 1 она нулевая, у копии шага 2 нулевой вход, и выходит 0. Весь градиент — это ошибка шага 2, пришедшая к копии шага 1 через \(v\): слагаемое «из будущего» в \(\delta^1\).

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад · вопрос 4 из 5 · открыть в уроке

What is truncated Backpropagation Through Time? What is gained and what is lost with respect to full BPTT? What is carried across the boundary between two chunks, and what is not?

Show model answer

The training sequence is split into consecutive chunks of \(U\) steps. Within each chunk ordinary BPTT is run (forward through the \(U\) steps, backward through the same \(U\) steps) and the weights are updated. The hidden state at the end of a chunk is passed on as the initial state of the next chunk, so the state is not reset; the gradient is not propagated across the boundary (in PyTorch: h = h.detach()).

Gains: memory \(O(U)\) instead of \(O(T)\), cheaper backward passes and more frequent updates. Loss: an error can assign credit only to steps of its own chunk, so dependencies longer than \(U\) steps receive no gradient. The network may still carry such information forward, but it cannot be trained to do so from these examples: learning is biased toward short-term dependencies. The \(U\) of the BPTT slides is this window; \(U = T\) gives full BPTT.

Две частые ошибки: «на каждом куске состояние обнуляют» (нет, отрезают только градиент) и «TBPTT — безобидное ускорение» (нет, длинные связи перестают учиться). Последняя фраза ответа связывает его со слайдом профессора.

Урок 30 · Перемотка назад · вопрос 5 из 5 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about Backpropagation Through Time are correct? Select all that apply.

  • The gradient of a shared weight is the sum of the gradients of all its replicas. Производная сложной функции многих переменных: каждая копия зависит от веса с производной 1.
  • The shared weights are updated at every time step of the backward pass. Шаг обучения один — после перемотки (в TBPTT — после каждого куска).
  • The forward pass has to store the hidden states of all unrolled steps. Шаг назад читает \(h^t\) (наклон) и \(h^{t-1}\) (кусок копии \(v\)).
  • Unrolling a network for \(U\) steps multiplies its number of trainable parameters by \(U\). Копии — тот же вес; параметров столько же, сколько у одной ячейки.
  • In truncated BPTT the hidden state is reset to zero at the beginning of every chunk. Состояние передаётся дальше; отрезают только историю градиента.
  • Averaging the replica gradients instead of summing them changes only the effective learning rate. Среднее — это сумма, умноженная на \(1/U\): то же направление, шаг \(\eta / U\).
Show model answer

Correct: the first, third and sixth statements. One update follows the whole backward pass; unrolling creates replicas of the same parameters, not new ones; truncation cuts the gradient at the chunk boundary but carries the state forward.

Неверные варианты — три типичные путаницы этого урока: «копии — это новые веса», «шаг на каждом шаге перемотки» и «TBPTT обнуляет состояние».

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки · вопрос 1 из 5 · открыть в уроке

Consider the simplified recurrent network seen in class, \(h^t = h(v^{(1)} h^{t-1} + w^{(1)} x^t)\), \(y^t = g(w^{(2)} h^t)\), trained with backpropagation through time on a sequence of length \(S\). (a) Write \(\partial E / \partial w\) as a sum over time and \(\partial E^t / \partial w\) as a sum over the earlier time steps. (b) Express \(\partial h^t / \partial h^k\) and derive an upper bound on its norm. (c) What does the bound imply for sigmoid and tanh units, and what does it not imply?

Show model answer

(a) The error of the sequence is \(E = \sum_t E^t\). For a weight \(w\) of the recurrent unit (\(w^{(1)}\) or \(v^{(1)}\)), \(E^t\) depends on the copy of \(w\) used at every step \(k \le t\):

\[ \pd{E}{w} = \sum_{t=1}^{S} \pd{E^t}{w} \] \[ \pd{E^t}{w} = \sum_{k=1}^{t} \pd{E^t}{y^t}\, \pd{y^t}{h^t}\, \pd{h^t}{h^k}\, \pd{h^k}{w} \]

where the last factor is the immediate derivative of \(h^k\) with respect to the copy of \(w\) used at step \(k\).

(b) With \(a^i = v^{(1)} h^{i-1} + w^{(1)} x^i\), the chain rule gives \(t - k\) factors:

\[ \begin{aligned} \pd{h^t}{h^k} &= \prod_{i=k+1}^{t} \pd{h^i}{h^{i-1}} \\ &= \prod_{i=k+1}^{t} v^{(1)}\, h'(a^i) \end{aligned} \]

Each factor is bounded, and so is their product:

\[ \lVert v^{(1)} h'(a^i) \rVert \le \lVert v^{(1)} \rVert\, \lVert h'(\cdot) \rVert = \gamma_v\, \gamma_{h'} \] \[ \Big\lVert \pd{h^t}{h^k} \Big\rVert \le (\gamma_v\, \gamma_{h'})^{t-k} \]

(c) \(\gamma_{h'} = 1/4\) for the sigmoid and \(1\) for tanh. If \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\) (\(|v^{(1)}| \lt 4\) for the sigmoid, \(|v^{(1)}| \lt 1\) for tanh), the terms with a large lag \(t - k\) go to zero exponentially: they are swamped by the short-lag terms, so the network learns only short-term dependencies (vanishing gradient). The bound is only a sufficient condition: \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \gt 1\) does not imply exploding gradients, because saturated units have \(h' \approx 0\).

В ответе важны три вещи: сумма по \(k\) (на слайде 13 лекции 4 её индекс напечатан как \(t\) — пиши \(k\)), ровно \(t - k\) множителей с одним и тем же \(v^{(1)}\) и правильное чтение оценки: она гарантирует затухание при \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\) и ничего не обещает выше. Числа \(1/4\) и \(1\) для \(\gamma_{h'}\) нужно знать наизусть.

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки · вопрос 2 из 5 · открыть в уроке

A tanh RNN has a recurrent weight matrix with spectral norm \(\lVert V \rVert = 0.8\). Using the bound \((\gamma_v \gamma_{h'})^{t-k}\), find the smallest lag \(t - k\) for which the bound guarantees that \(\lVert \partial h^t / \partial h^k \rVert\) is below \(10^{-3}\).

Show model answer

For tanh \(\gamma_{h'} = 1\), so \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} = 0.8\). We need \(0.8^{n} \lt 10^{-3}\):

\[ n \gt \frac{\ln 10^{-3}}{\ln 0.8} \approx 30.96 \quad\Rightarrow\quad n = 31 \]

Check: \(0.8^{30} \approx 1.24 \cdot 10^{-3}\), \(0.8^{31} \approx 0.99 \cdot 10^{-3}\).

Типичная ошибка — забыть, что \(\ln 0.8 \lt 0\), и перевернуть неравенство не туда. Проверь ответ прямой подстановкой: калькулятор это позволяет. Для sigmoid с той же нормой хватило бы 5 шагов: \(0.2^{5} = 3.2 \cdot 10^{-4}\).

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки · вопрос 3 из 5 · открыть в уроке

Select all correct statements about a vanilla RNN trained with backpropagation through time.

  • If \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\), the contribution of step \(k\) to the gradient at step \(t\) vanishes exponentially in \(t - k\). Каждый из \(t - k\) множителей не больше \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\): это вывод слайда 13 лекции 4.
  • If \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \gt 1\), the gradients explode. Оценка — потолок. У tanh с \(v = 2\) в насыщении потолок \(2^{10}\), а на деле \(1.6 \cdot 10^{-8}\).
  • With sigmoid units, vanishing is guaranteed for every \(|v^{(1)}| \lt 4\). \(\gamma_{h'} = 1/4\), поэтому \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\) при \(|v^{(1)}| \lt 4\).
  • Gradient clipping solves the vanishing gradient problem. Clipping только укорачивает длинный градиент. Крошечные дальние слагаемые он не увеличит.
  • A vanishing gradient means that the gradient of the recurrent weight is close to zero. Градиент может быть большим: его дают ближние шаги. Исчезают дальние слагаемые суммы.
  • The linear unit \(h^t = h^{t-1} + w^{(1)} x^t\) passes the gradient back through any number of steps unchanged. Каждый множитель \(1 \cdot 1\), поэтому \(\partial h^t / \partial h^k = 1\).
Show model answer

Correct: the first, third and sixth statements. The bound \((\gamma_v \gamma_{h'})^{t-k}\) is an upper bound, so it proves vanishing when \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\) and proves nothing when it is above 1. Clipping can only shrink the gradient. With vanishing gradients the total gradient is dominated by the short-lag terms; it is the long-lag terms that vanish. A linear unit with self-weight 1 has all factors equal to 1.

Две ловушки: второе утверждение (из растущего потолка рост не следует) и пятое (затухание — это не «градиент ноль», а «дальние слагаемые ноль»). Если пятое кажется верным, вернись к столбикам второго виджета.

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки · вопрос 4 из 5 · открыть в уроке

What are exploding gradients in a recurrent network, how do they show up during training, and why are they considered easier to deal with than vanishing gradients? Describe gradient clipping by norm (give the formula) and say what it does not solve.

Show model answer

Each long-lag term of the gradient contains the product of \(t - k\) per-step factors \(v^{(1)} h'(a^i)\) (in general the Jacobians \(D^i V\)). When the actual factors are larger than 1 in absolute value, for example in a linear or active ReLU unit with \(|v^{(1)}| \gt 1\), the product grows exponentially with the lag and the gradient explodes. The bound \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \gt 1\) is only a necessary condition for this, not a sufficient one. During training the loss suddenly spikes, the weight updates become huge, and the values finally overflow to inf and NaN.

They are easier to deal with because they are visible and can be cut directly. Gradient clipping by norm rescales the whole gradient vector \(g\) when its norm exceeds a threshold \(\tau\):

\[ g \leftarrow g \cdot \min\Big(1,\ \frac{\tau}{\lVert g \rVert}\Big) \]

The direction is kept and only the step length is capped (clipping each component separately would change the direction). It is applied after backpropagation and before the optimizer step.

Clipping does nothing for vanishing gradients: it can only shrink the gradient, and the tiny long-range terms are not recovered. That needs a different architecture with a memory path of gain 1, as in the LSTM.

Полный ответ — три части: механизм (настоящие множители больше 1, произведение растёт с лагом), симптомы (скачки ошибки, NaN) и формула clipping по норме с оговоркой, что направление сохраняется. Две фразы-ошибки: "clipping helps with vanishing gradients" и "\(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \gt 1\) means exploding gradients".

Урок 31 · Микрофон у колонки · вопрос 5 из 5 · открыть в уроке

To fix the simplified recurrent network \(h^t = h(v^{(1)} h^{t-1} + w^{(1)} x^t)\), the lecture replaces it with a linear unit with \(v^{(1)} = 1\). Explain why this removes the vanishing gradient, why both changes (linear activation and \(v^{(1)} = 1\)) are needed, and what problem remains.

Show model answer

With \(h^t = h^{t-1} + w^{(1)} x^t\) every factor is \(v^{(1)} h' = 1 \cdot 1\), so \(\partial h^t / \partial h^k = 1\) for every lag: the error flows back through any number of steps without vanishing or exploding (linear memory update).

Both changes are needed. With \(v^{(1)} = 1\) but a tanh activation the factor of step \(i\) is \(1 - (h^i)^2 \lt 1\) as soon as the state is not zero, and close to 0 in saturation. With a linear activation but \(v^{(1)} \ne 1\) the product is \((v^{(1)})^{t-k}\), which vanishes or explodes.

The remaining problem: the unit only accumulates the input. It cannot forget, cannot choose what to store, and its value can grow without bound (with \(w^{(1)} = 1\), a constant input 1 gives 1, 2, 3, …). The LSTM builds its memory cell on this linear loop and adds multiplicative gates that decide when to write, keep and read.

Опорные фразы — облака слайда 14 лекции 4: "Linear memory update" и "It only accumulates the input". Ответ без второй половины — почему такого нейрона мало — неполон. Про ворота хватит одной фразы: подробно они в следующем уроке.

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Write the equations of an LSTM cell in the order used in class: input gate, candidate, forget gate, cell state, output gate, hidden state. For each quantity give its activation function, the range of its values and its role. Which of these quantities is passed to the next layer?

Show model answer
\[ i_t = \sigma\big(W_i \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t] + b_i\big) \] \[ \tilde C_t = \tanh\big(W_C \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t] + b_C\big) \] \[ f_t = \sigma\big(W_f \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t] + b_f\big) \] \[ C_t = f_t * C_{t-1} + i_t * \tilde C_t \] \[ o_t = \sigma\big(W_o \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t] + b_o\big) \] \[ h_t = o_t * \tanh(C_t) \]

\([h_{t-1}, x_t]\) is the concatenation of the previous hidden state and the current input, \(*\) is the element-wise product, and every gate is a vector with one value per memory unit.

Input (write) gate \(i_t\): sigmoid, in (0, 1); how much of the candidate is written. Candidate \(\tilde C_t\): tanh, in (−1, 1); the signed content proposed for writing. Forget (keep) gate \(f_t\): sigmoid, in (0, 1); how much of each component of \(C_{t-1}\) is kept (1 = keep, 0 = erase). Cell state \(C_t\): additive gated update, not bounded; the long-term memory, passed only to the same cell at the next time step. Output (read) gate \(o_t\): sigmoid, in (0, 1); how much of the memory is exposed. Hidden state \(h_t\): in (−1, 1); the short-term state and the output of the cell.

Only \(h_t\) goes to the next layer (or to the output layer); it also enters the four blocks at time \(t+1\) as \(h_{t-1}\).

Опора ответа: порядок строк как на слайде 17, сигмоида против tanh, диапазоны и слово additive в обновлении \(C_t\). Две частые ошибки: назвать кандидата «четвёртыми воротами» со значениями от 0 до 1 и написать, что наружу выходит \(C_t\). Если в вопросе встретится "memory gate", это строка с \(C_t\).

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Explain why an LSTM suffers much less than a vanilla RNN from the vanishing gradient problem. Compare the factor that multiplies the gradient at each time step along the memory path in the two models. Does the LSTM eliminate the problem?

Show model answer

Vanilla RNN (one unit, as in class): going back one step multiplies the gradient by \(v^{(1)} h'(\cdot)\), so

\[ \Big\lvert \frac{\partial h^t}{\partial h^{t-k}} \Big\rvert \le (\gamma_v \gamma_{h'})^{k} , \]

which vanishes exponentially when \(\gamma_v \gamma_{h'} \lt 1\). Since \(\sigma' \le 1/4\) and \(\tanh' \le 1\), with equality only at 0, this is the usual case with sigmoids and tanh.

LSTM: \(C_t = f_t * C_{t-1} + i_t * \tilde C_t\). Along the cell state (direct path, gates held fixed) \(\partial C_t / \partial C_{t-1} = \operatorname{diag}(f_t)\): no weight matrix and no derivative of a squashing function, only the forget-gate values, which the network chooses at every step. Over \(k\) steps the factor is the element-wise product of the forget gates. When the cell keeps \(f \approx 1\) the error flows back almost unchanged (constant error carousel); in the original 1997 cell the loop weight was fixed to 1, so the error along the carousel was exactly constant.

The LSTM mitigates the problem but does not eliminate it: the product still decays when \(f \lt 1\) (\(0.9^{100} \approx 2.7 \cdot 10^{-5}\)); the paths through the gates and \(h_{t-1}\) behave like an RNN and can still vanish or explode, so gradient clipping is still used. In practice the forget-gate bias is initialised to about 1.

Нужны два множителя рядом (\(v^{(1)} h'\) против \(f_t\)), слова direct path или along the cell state и честное «mitigates». Ответ «LSTM solves both vanishing and exploding gradients» неверен во второй половине и спорен в первой.

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

An LSTM layer with two memory units has \(C_{t-1} = (3,\ {-2})\). At time \(t\) the gates are \(f_t = (0.8,\ 0.1)\), \(i_t = (0.5,\ 0.9)\), \(o_t = (0.5,\ 1)\), and the candidate is \(\tilde C_t = ({-0.4},\ 0.5)\). Compute \(C_t\) and \(h_t\) and enter the first component of \(h_t\). Which unit preserves its memory and which overwrites it?

Show model answer
\[ C_{t,1} = 0.8 \cdot 3 + 0.5 \cdot (-0.4) = 2.2 \] \[ C_{t,2} = 0.1 \cdot (-2) + 0.9 \cdot 0.5 = 0.25 \] \[ h_{t,1} = 0.5 \cdot \tanh 2.2 = 0.488 \] \[ h_{t,2} = 1 \cdot \tanh 0.25 = 0.245 \]

Unit 1 preserves its memory (it keeps 80% and adds a small negative correction); its content is half hidden by \(o = 0.5\). Unit 2 overwrites it (it keeps 10% and writes 90% of a positive candidate): its sign flips from −2 to 0.25.

Типичные ошибки: забыть \(o_t\) и ответить 0.976; взять tanh от \(C_{t-1}\) вместо \(C_t\) (выйдет 0.498); перемножить векторы как матрицы, а не поэлементно.

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

How many trainable parameters does a single LSTM layer have with input size \(d = 16\) and hidden size \(n = 32\), using the equations seen in class (one bias vector per gate)? How many does PyTorch's nn.LSTM(16, 32) have, and why?

Show model answer

Four blocks (\(i\), \(\tilde C\), \(f\), \(o\)), each with \(W \in \R^{n \times (n+d)}\) and \(b \in \R^{n}\):

\[ 4\,\big(32 \cdot 48 + 32\big) = 6272 . \]

PyTorch keeps two bias vectors per block (b_ih and b_hh): \(4\,(32 \cdot 16 + 32 \cdot 32 + 2 \cdot 32) = 6400\). The count does not depend on the length of the sequence.

Частая ошибка — забыть, что матрица действует на \([h_{t-1}, x_t]\), и посчитать \(n \times d\) вместо \(n \times (n + d)\): выйдет 2176. Если в задаче не сказано, сколько смещений, назови соглашение явно.

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

The forget gate of one memory unit stays at \(f = 0.95\) for 50 consecutive time steps. What fraction of the error signal reaches the cell state 50 steps back along the cell-state path? What would it be with \(f = 0.5\), the typical value of a freshly initialised forget gate with zero bias?

Show model answer

Along the cell state each step multiplies the gradient by \(f\):

\[ 0.95^{50} \approx 0.0769 , \qquad 0.5^{50} \approx 8.9 \cdot 10^{-16} . \]

With \(f = 0.5\) the carousel is useless; this is why the forget-gate bias is initialised to a positive value, typically 1, giving \(f \approx \sigma(1) \approx 0.73\) at the start of training.

Суть задачи: множители по ленте перемножаются, а не складываются, и «constant» в CEC держится только при \(f\) около 1.

Урок 32 · Тетрадь, которую не переписывают · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Select all correct statements about the LSTM.

  • The hidden state \(h_t\) is bounded in (−1, 1), while the cell state \(C_t\) is not bounded. \(h_t = o_t * \tanh(C_t)\) с \(o_t\) от 0 до 1; \(C_t\) может расти почти на единицу за шаг.
  • The output gate \(o_t\) scales the content that is stored in \(C_t\). \(o_t\) в формулу для \(C_t\) не входит: он только решает, сколько памяти показать.
  • The original LSTM of Hochreiter and Schmidhuber (1997) already had a forget gate. В 1997 году ворот было двое, input и output, вес петли 1.0; forget gate появился в 2000 году (Gers и соавторы).
  • Each gate is a vector with one value per memory unit. Ворота считаются поэлементно: каждая ячейка памяти решает за себя.
  • Thanks to the constant error carousel, an LSTM never needs gradient clipping. Пути через ворота и \(h\) устроены как в RNN и могут взрываться; clipping используют и для LSTM.
  • Along the cell state, the gradient over \(k\) steps is the element-wise product of the \(k\) forget gates. Верно для прямого пути по ленте: \(\operatorname{diag}(f)\) на каждом шаге.
  • The candidate \(\tilde C_t\) is a fourth gate with values in (0, 1). Кандидат — tanh со значениями от −1 до 1: это содержимое записи, а не ворота.
Show model answer

Correct: the first, fourth and sixth statements. \(o_t\) affects only \(h_t\); the 1997 cell had input and output gates and a loop of fixed weight 1.0; exploding gradients remain possible through the gate paths, so clipping is still used; the candidate uses tanh and takes values in (−1, 1).

Ловушки — третья (история ворот) и пятая: из «constant error carousel» не следует, что градиент не может взорваться.

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Write the equations of a GRU cell as in the course slides. Which components of the LSTM are merged or removed, and what is added? Using the slides' convention, what happens to the state when \(z_t = 0\), and to the candidate when \(r_t = 0\)?

Show model answer
\[ z_t = \sigma(W_z \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t]) \] \[ r_t = \sigma(W_r \cdot [h_{t-1}, x_t]) \] \[ \tilde h_t = \tanh(W \cdot [r_t * h_{t-1}, x_t]) \] \[ h_t = (1 - z_t) * h_{t-1} + z_t * \tilde h_t \]

Here \(*\) is the element-wise product; biases are omitted as on the slide. Compared with the LSTM, the forget and input gates are merged into the update gate \(z_t\) (\(z_t\) plays the input gate, \(1 - z_t\) the forget gate), and the cell state and the hidden state are merged into \(h_t\). There is no output gate, so the whole state is exposed. A reset gate \(r_t\) is added: it decides how much of \(h_{t-1}\) enters the candidate. The cell has 3 weight blocks instead of 4.

\(z_t = 0\): \(h_t = h_{t-1}\), the state is copied unchanged, and the gradient along this path passes with factor 1. \(r_t = 0\): the candidate ignores the past and depends only on \(x_t\); \(h_t\) still mixes it with \(h_{t-1}\) through \(z_t\). In PyTorch and in Cho et al. (2014) the roles of \(z_t\) and \(1 - z_t\) are swapped.

В хорошем ответе три вещи: четыре уравнения с \(r_t\) в правильном месте (внутри кандидата, а не в формуле для \(h_t\)), карту «что слили, что убрали, что добавили» и смысл крайних значений. Типичные ошибки — назвать reset gate «аналогом input gate» и не сказать, в каком соглашении записан \(z\).

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

A GRU with two hidden units has \(h_{t-1} = (0.6,\ {-0.4})\). At time \(t\) the update gate is \(z_t = (0.1,\ 0.9)\) and the candidate is \(\tilde h_t = (-0.5,\ 0.8)\). Using \(h_t = (1 - z_t) * h_{t-1} + z_t * \tilde h_t\), compute \(h_t\) and enter its second component.

Show model answer
\[ h_{t,1} = 0.9 \cdot 0.6 + 0.1 \cdot (-0.5) = 0.49 \] \[ h_{t,2} = 0.1 \cdot (-0.4) + 0.9 \cdot 0.8 = 0.68 \]

\(h_t = (0.49,\ 0.68)\). Unit 1 mostly keeps its past value (\(z = 0.1\)); unit 2 is mostly overwritten by the candidate (\(z = 0.9\)).

Если подставить числа в формулу PyTorch, где \(z\) сохраняет прошлое, выйдет \(({-0.39},\ {-0.28})\). Считай по формуле слайда и выпиши её перед подстановкой: так видно, какое соглашение взято.

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

How many trainable parameters does a single-layer GRU have with input size \(d = 10\) and hidden size \(n = 20\), with one bias vector per weight block as in the equations of the notes? How many does PyTorch's nn.GRU(10, 20) have, and why?

Show model answer

Three blocks (\(z\), \(r\), \(\tilde h\)), each with a matrix \(W \in \R^{n \times (n + d)}\) acting on a vector of size \(n + d\) (\([h_{t-1}, x_t]\), or \([r_t * h_{t-1}, x_t]\) for the candidate) and a bias \(b \in \R^n\):

\[ 3\,(20 \cdot 30 + 20) = 3 \cdot 620 = 1860 \]

Without biases, as on the slide, it is \(3 \cdot 20 \cdot 30 = 1800\). PyTorch keeps two bias vectors per block (b_ih and b_hh): \(3\,(20 \cdot 30 + 2 \cdot 20) = 1920\). With the same bias convention a GRU has exactly three quarters of the parameters of an LSTM of the same size (3 blocks instead of 4). The count does not depend on the sequence length.

Ловушка — забыть, что блок читает склейку \([h_{t-1}, x_t]\) размера \(n + d\), а не один вход \(x_t\): тогда выходит 660. Если в задаче не сказано, сколько смещений, назови соглашение явно.

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

What is a bidirectional RNN, how is its representation at time \(t\) formed, and when can it not be used? Can a bidirectional LSTM be used for (a) part-of-speech tagging of a written sentence, (b) a next-word language model, (c) forecasting the next 24 hours of a time series from the last 7 days?

Show model answer

Two independent RNNs with separate weights run over the same input: one left-to-right, giving \(\overrightarrow{h_t}\), one right-to-left, giving \(\overleftarrow{h_t}\). The representation at time \(t\) is the concatenation \(h_t = [\overrightarrow{h_t} ; \overleftarrow{h_t}] \in \R^{2n}\). Since \(\overrightarrow{h_t}\) depends on \(x_1, \dots, x_t\) and \(\overleftarrow{h_t}\) on \(x_t, \dots, x_T\), every \(h_t\) is conditioned on the whole sequence.

It can be used only when conditioning on the full input sequence, i.e. when the whole input is available before the outputs are needed. It cannot be used for online/streaming prediction or for autoregressive generation.

(a) Yes: the whole sentence is known. (b) No: \(\overleftarrow{h_t}\) would already contain the word to be predicted, and at generation time the future does not exist. (c) Yes for encoding the observed 7-day window, which is entirely in the past; the part that emits the forecast step by step must be causal.

Ключевые слова — "two independent RNNs", "concatenation", "conditioning on the full input sequence". Пункт (c) — ловушка: ответ «прогноз, значит, нельзя» неверен. Сильный ответ разделяет модель на части: encoder окна может быть двунаправленным, decoder прогноза — нет.

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

How can the initial state of an RNN be set? Explain how a learned initial state is trained. Does it allow the network to start differently for different kinds of sequences?

Show model answer

Either fix it (typically \(h_0 = 0\), and \(C_0 = 0\) for an LSTM) or treat it as learned parameters: start from random guesses of the initial state values, backpropagate the prediction error through time all the way to the initial state, compute the gradient of the error with respect to it, and update it by gradient descent like any other weight.

It costs \(2n\) parameters per layer and direction for an LSTM (\(n\) for a GRU) and helps mostly short sequences and the first time steps. It does not give a different start per kind of sequence: after training it is one vector shared by all sequences, a dataset-level prior. A per-sequence start requires computing \(h_0\) from side information \(c\), \(h_0 = f(c)\): e.g. an image encoding in captioning or the encoder's last state in seq2seq.

Три шага слайда стоит воспроизвести почти дословно. Последний вопрос проверяет, понимаешь ли ты, что выученный вектор после обучения — константа.

Урок 33 · Глубже и в обе стороны · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which statements about a stacked (multi-layer) LSTM are correct? Select all that apply.

  • At time \(t\), layer \(\ell \ge 2\) receives \(h_t^{(\ell-1)}\), the hidden state of the layer below at the same time step. Вход снизу — с того же шага; своё прошлое слой берёт слева.
  • The weights are shared across time steps within a layer, and each layer has its own weights. \(W^{(1)} \ne W^{(2)}\), а внутри слоя одни и те же на всех шагах.
  • Layer \(\ell \ge 2\) receives the cell state \(C_t^{(\ell-1)}\) of the layer below. Память \(C\) идёт только вправо, к следующему шагу своего слоя; наверх уходит \(h\).
  • Doubling the sequence length doubles the number of parameters. Веса общие для всех шагов: длина на число параметров не влияет.
  • In the course slides, a ReLU layer sits between the top LSTM layer and the output \(Y_t\). Так нарисовано на слайдах 24 и 26 лекции 4.
Show model answer

Correct: 1, 2 and 5. The cell state stays inside its layer and only moves forward in time, and the parameter count is independent of \(T\). Stacking gives a hierarchical representation: higher layers capture slower, more abstract temporal patterns.

Ловушки здесь две: какой индекс времени у входа слоя сверху (\(t\), а не \(t - 1\)) и что передаётся наверх (\(h\), а не \(C\)).

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 1 из 6 · открыть в уроке

For each task, state its input/output structure (one-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-one, many-to-many non-synced, synced many-to-many) and say at which time steps the loss is computed: (a) sentiment of a tweet; (b) image captioning; (c) part-of-speech tagging; (d) English-to-French translation; (e) labelling every frame of a video.

Show model answer

(a) Many-to-one: the RNN reads the whole tweet and the output is read only from the last state, \(\hat y = g(h_T)\); one loss term, at the last step.

(b) One-to-many: the CNN feature vector of the image initialises the RNN (or is its first input); one word is generated per step and fed back as the next input; one loss term per generated word, including the end token <eos>.

(c) Synced many-to-many (sequence labelling): one tag per word, \(\hat y_t = g(h_t)\); a loss term at every step, \(E = \sum_t E^t\). Since the whole sentence is available, a bidirectional RNN can be used.

(d) Non-synced many-to-many (encoder–decoder): the source sentence is read first, with no outputs and no loss; then the translation is generated word by word; a loss term at every output step, including <eos>.

(e) Synced many-to-many: one label per frame, a loss term at every frame.

Две типичные ошибки: забыть <eos> среди слагаемых ошибки и поставить ошибку на шаги чтения в переводе. Двунаправленная сеть в (c) уместна только с оговоркой «предложение известно целиком».

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 2 из 6 · открыть в уроке

In machine translation the source and the target sentence sometimes have the same number of words. Explain, with an example, why a synced many-to-many RNN (one output per input step) is still not suitable for translation, and describe the structure that is used instead.

Show model answer

Synced means a one-to-one, order-preserving correspondence between input and output steps, not just equal length. A unidirectional synced RNN must emit \(y_t\) when it has read only \(x_1, \dots, x_t\). In "the red car" → "la macchina rossa" both sentences have three words, but the second Italian word ("macchina") translates the third English word ("car"): at step 2 the network has read only "the red" and cannot know the noun ("the red apple" → "la mela rossa"). A bidirectional RNN would see the whole source, but a synced network still emits exactly one word per source word, while in general the lengths differ ("Я видел котю на мате" → "I saw a cat on a mat": 5 → 7 words).

Translation is a non-synced many-to-many problem: the network first reads the whole source sentence (no outputs, no loss), then generates the target word by word, feeding each generated word back as the next input, until an end-of-sequence token. This is the encoder–decoder: an RNN "reads a sentence in English and then outputs a sentence in French".

В ответе нужны три вещи: определение «в ногу» через порядок, а не через длину; конкретный шаг, на котором ломается сеть, читающая слева направо; и разные длины, от которых не спасает и двунаправленная сеть. Пример годится любой, где порядок слов в двух языках разный.

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 3 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Which of the following statements are correct? Select all that apply.

  • In a many-to-one RNN classifier whose output is computed from the last hidden state only, the gradient of the loss reaches the first input only by back-propagating through all the time steps. Ошибка одна и стоит в конце, поэтому путь к \(x_1\) проходит через все \(T\) шагов.
  • If the source and the target sequence have the same length, the problem is synced (aligned). Нужен ещё и порядок: «Я тебя люблю» → "I love you".
  • A bidirectional RNN can be used to tag the words of a written sentence, but not in the part of a captioning model that generates the words. Для разметки предложение известно целиком; при генерации следующих слов ещё нет.
  • In image captioning the end-of-sequence token is only needed at inference time, so it does not appear in the training loss. <eos> — один из правильных ответов при обучении, иначе сеть не научится останавливаться.
  • Activity recognition from a fixed-length window of accelerometer samples, read step by step by an LSTM, is a many-to-one problem. Постоянная длина не делает вход одним объектом: сеть читает окно по шагам.
Show model answer

Correct: A, C, E. B is false: equal length is not enough, the correspondence must also preserve the order. D is false: <eos> is a training target, otherwise the model never learns when to stop.

Ловушки этого вопроса — пункты B и D: «в ногу = одинаковой длины» и «<eos> нужен только при генерации».

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 4 из 6 · открыть в уроке

An encoder–decoder RNN is trained on the pair: source "Я видел котю на мате <eos>" (5 words and the end token), target "I saw a cat on a mat <eos>". How many per-step loss terms does this training pair contribute to the loss of the sequence?

Show model answer

Loss terms exist only where an output is compared with a target, i.e. on the decoder side, one per target token: 7 words plus <eos> give 8 terms. The 6 encoder steps produce no output and no loss.

Частые неверные ответы: 7 (забыт <eos>) и 14 (посчитаны ещё и шаги чтения).

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 5 из 6 · открыть в уроке

A student claims: "Only a recurrent network can generate a sequence of arbitrary length from a single input; a feed-forward network cannot." Is the claim correct? Explain what is actually needed to generate variable-length sequences and what recurrence adds.

Show model answer

Not correct. Generating a sequence of arbitrary length needs (1) a model of the next element given the input and the previous elements, \(p(y_t \mid y_{\lt t}, x)\), and (2) an autoregressive loop: each generated element is fed back as the next input until the model emits an end-of-sequence token. Any next-element predictor can be run this way, including a feed-forward network that sees a fixed window of the last few elements, such as Bengio's Neural Net Language Model (NNLM); the lecture lists feed-forward networks among the models for sequences.

Recurrence adds memory: the hidden state, of fixed size and with weights shared over time, summarises the whole prefix however long it is, while a window-based network forgets everything older than its window.

Ответ «нет, нужны цикл и <eos>» без слова про память неполон: вторая половина вопроса — что даёт рекуррентность.

Урок 34 · Пять форм задач · вопрос 6 из 6 · открыть в уроке

Given an image \(x\), a captioning model assigns \(P(y_1 = \text{Two} \mid x) = 0.5\), \(P(y_2 = \text{dogs} \mid \text{Two}, x) = 0.4\) and \(P(y_3 = \langle eos\rangle \mid \text{Two dogs}, x) = 0.9\). What probability does the model assign to the caption "Two dogs"?

Show model answer
\[ P = 0.5 \cdot 0.4 \cdot 0.9 = 0.18 \]

The end token is part of the sequence. Without its factor, 0.2 is the probability that the caption starts with "Two dogs", not that it is exactly "Two dogs".

Ответ 0.2 — типичная ошибка: забыт множитель <eos>.
